# Klip TUI Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** `klip` with no arguments opens a full-screen terminal app (Home, Devices, Pair, History, Send, Log, Settings) that does everything the subcommands do, plus the additive daemon calls the spec asks for; every existing subcommand keeps working exactly as before.

**Architecture:** A new `klip-tui` crate holds an Elm-style core (pure `update(state, msg) -> effects`, a `view` that only draws, a runtime that executes effects against a `DaemonApi` trait). `klip-cli` implements that trait over the existing Unix-socket client and opens the TUI when started on a terminal without arguments. The control protocol grows append-only (`Hello`, `UnpairAll`, `CopyToClipboard`, `ClearHistory`), and the daemon reads requests without trusting the announced length.

**Tech Stack:** Rust 2021 (workspace `rust-version = 1.91`), `ratatui =0.30.2` (feature `crossterm` only), `crossterm =0.29.0` (`event-stream`), `tokio`, `async-trait`, `futures-util`, `unicode-width`, `unicode-segmentation`, `qrcode`; tests: `portable-pty =0.9.0`, `vt100 =0.16.2`, `rand`.

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-13-klip-tui-design.md` (read it first; this plan implements it). Deliberate deviations from the spec, all improvements found while planning:
1. Backend work (protocol, daemon handlers, request cap, CLI parity) comes **first** (Phase A) because the `DaemonApi` trait uses the new types. Milestone exit criteria are unchanged.
2. `unpair_all` works against old daemons too: the CLI-side implementation falls back to one `Unpair` per device, so only `copy_to_clipboard` and `clear_history` are greyed out on a v0.3.6 daemon.
3. Stale replies are prevented by "one request in flight per poll kind" plus a `session` number for pairing, instead of generation numbers everywhere.
4. Mouse support is: click a tab, wheel scrolls lists/previews (no per-row click).
5. Zero-width joiner/non-joiner (U+200C/U+200D) are **not** marked (they are needed for emoji and several scripts); U+200B, U+2060, U+FEFF, bidi controls and tag characters (U+E0000–E007F) are.
6. `TERM=dumb` (or unset) is treated like "no terminal": help and exit 2.
7. Characterization covers `--help` texts and no-daemon error output; `ui.rs` card renderers are not modified, which a `git diff --stat` check in Task 5 proves, so no renderer goldens are added.
8. The workspace package version is stale (`0.1.0` while releases are `0.3.6`); it is set to `0.3.6` in Task 4 so `Hello` reports a true daemon version, and to `0.4.0` at release.

## Global Constraints

- TUI code compiles only on `cfg(unix)`; Windows keeps compiling without it. macOS is compile-checked only.
- `klip` with no arguments opens the TUI **only** when stdin and stdout are terminals and `TERM` is set and not `dumb`; otherwise it prints help and exits with code 2, exactly as today.
- Every existing `klip <subcommand>` keeps its behaviour; only `unpair` and `history` gain options (`--all`, `--yes`, `history clear`).
- `postcard` enums are index-encoded: new `IpcRequest`/`IpcResponse` variants are appended **at the end**, never reordered or changed; a golden-bytes test pins this.
- Clip text, device names, log lines and file names are untrusted: they go through `sanitize` before they reach the screen. Secret items show `•••• secret (N B)` until revealed per item.
- `config.json` is written atomically (temp file mode 0600 → fsync → rename) and only after `DaemonConfig::validate()` passes; a malformed existing file is never overwritten silently.
- The terminal is restored on every exit path: normal quit, Ctrl+C, SIGTERM, SIGHUP, panic, daemon death.
- Minimum terminal 60 × 16; smaller shows a "terminal too small" screen.
- Intervals: status 1 s; pair poll 500 ms while `status.pairing_open`; history on open / when `total_synced_clips` changes / `r` / every 10 s while visible; log tail 500 ms while visible; reconnect 1 s; UI tick 250 ms.
- Limits: editor 1 MiB; history preview 256 KiB; log keeps 5,000 lines and reads the last 256 KiB at open; a loaded file for Send is capped at 64 MiB.
- Request size: the daemon accepts `max_clip_bytes + 64 KiB` per request (`klip_proto::ipc::max_request_bytes`); old daemons accept 8 MiB (`OLD_DAEMON_REQUEST_CAP`).
- Dependency versions are pinned exactly (`=`) in the workspace manifest.
- Heavy work one at a time: before any build or test run check `free -m` (available RAM ≥ 2.5 GB, swap used < 2 GB); never run two cargo jobs at once; delete scratch build outputs when done.
- Commits are ledger entries (CLAUDE.md): `type(scope): summary` header, **Why**, **inventory** per file/module, **Verification / Impact** written from the real command output of that task (including what was *not* verified), trailer `Co-Authored-By: Claude Sonnet 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>`. Write the message to a file or heredoc (never inline backticks in `-m`). Never print secrets or keystore contents.
- README/docs state only what a test or a recorded manual run shows; everything else is written as "unverified".

## Review Focus

The spec implies these inputs and failures but no single feature test exercises them; each has a pinning test in the task named in brackets:

1. **Hostile clip text** (escape sequences, OSC titles, "leave the alternate screen", bidi overrides, tag characters, a 250 KB single line) appearing in History, device names, the Send view and the Log must never reach the terminal raw. [Task 7 `sanitize` tests; Task 12 hostile device-name snapshot; Task 16 History snapshots, the 250 KB item and the real-terminal test that a clip cannot leave the alternate screen; Task 17 Send snapshot; Task 19 Log with escape bytes]
2. **The daemon dies or restarts** while a confirm or approval modal is open, a send is in flight, or the pair window is open: the UI closes the modal with a plain notice, never hangs, and reconnects by itself. [Task 11 `losing_the_daemon…`; Task 15 `losing_the_daemon_resets…`; Task 17 `failures_and_a_dead_daemon…`; Task 18 stop/restart and "request waiting while the daemon dies"]
3. **Broken or odd files**: malformed `config.json`, a log that is missing, truncated, rotated or binary, a history item over 256 KiB whose content the daemon dropped. [Task 9 file tests; Task 16 `…too_large…`; Task 19 log harness test; Task 20 `a_malformed_config_…`]
4. **Odd terminals**: 1×1 and 500×200, resize during a modal or text field, `TERM=dumb`, stdout not a terminal, SIGTERM/SIGHUP/panic in raw mode. [Task 12 `too_small…`; Task 13 `resizing…1x1`; Task 14 PTY tests and key fuzz]
5. **Stale or foreign pairing**: a pairing window opened by `klip pair` in another terminal, a request that disappears before Approve, an in-flight poll that reports an already-answered request. [Task 15 `a_window_opened_elsewhere…`, `a_request_that_vanishes…`, session tests; Task 18 real daemons]

# Phase A — backend, additive and tested first

### Task 1: Characterization tests of today's CLI

Record what the CLI does **before** anything changes, so any later difference is deliberate and visible.

**Files:**
- Create: `crates/klip-cli/tests/cli_golden.rs`
- Create: `crates/klip-cli/tests/golden/*.txt` (generated)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: the `klip` binary (`CARGO_BIN_EXE_klip`).
- Produces: `check(name, args, want_code)` golden helper pattern (re-implemented in Task 5's tests; not shared).

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the test file**

```rust
//! Characterization tests: the CLI's help texts and its behaviour without a daemon or a terminal,
//! recorded before the TUI work started. A change here must be deliberate and visible in the diff.
//! Regenerate with `UPDATE_GOLDEN=1 cargo test -p klip-cli --test cli_golden` and review `git diff`.
use std::path::PathBuf;
use std::process::{Command, Stdio};

fn klip(args: &[&str]) -> (String, i32) {
    let dir = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("klip-golden-{}", std::process::id()));
    let out = Command::new(env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_klip"))
        .args(args)
        .stdin(Stdio::null())
        .env("KLIP_CONFIG_DIR", dir)
        .env_remove("TERM")
        .output()
        .expect("run klip");
    let mut text = String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stdout).into_owned();
    text.push_str(&String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stderr));
    (text, out.status.code().unwrap_or(-1))
}

fn golden_path(name: &str) -> PathBuf {
    PathBuf::from(env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR"))
        .join("tests/golden")
        .join(name)
}

fn check(name: &str, args: &[&str], want_code: i32) {
    let (text, code) = klip(args);
    assert_eq!(code, want_code, "exit code of `klip {}`", args.join(" "));
    let path = golden_path(name);
    if std::env::var_os("UPDATE_GOLDEN").is_some() {
        std::fs::create_dir_all(path.parent().unwrap()).unwrap();
        std::fs::write(&path, &text).unwrap();
        return;
    }
    let want = std::fs::read_to_string(&path)
        .unwrap_or_else(|_| panic!("missing {}; run with UPDATE_GOLDEN=1", path.display()));
    assert_eq!(text, want, "output of `klip {}` changed", args.join(" "));
}

#[test]
fn help_top_level() {
    check("help.txt", &["--help"], 0);
}

#[test]
fn help_of_every_subcommand() {
    for (name, args) in [
        ("help_start.txt", vec!["start", "--help"]),
        ("help_stop.txt", vec!["stop", "--help"]),
        ("help_status.txt", vec!["status", "--help"]),
        ("help_pair.txt", vec!["pair", "--help"]),
        ("help_unpair.txt", vec!["unpair", "--help"]),
        ("help_pause.txt", vec!["pause", "--help"]),
        ("help_resume.txt", vec!["resume", "--help"]),
        ("help_history.txt", vec!["history", "--help"]),
        ("help_send.txt", vec!["send", "--help"]),
        ("help_install_service.txt", vec!["install-service", "--help"]),
    ] {
        check(name, &args, 0);
    }
}

#[test]
fn no_arguments_without_a_terminal_prints_help_and_exits_2() {
    check("no_args.txt", &[], 2);
}

#[test]
fn commands_that_need_the_daemon_say_so_when_it_is_not_running() {
    for (name, args) in [
        ("down_status.txt", vec!["status"]),
        ("down_stop.txt", vec!["stop"]),
        ("down_history.txt", vec!["history"]),
        ("down_send.txt", vec!["send", "hello"]),
        ("down_pause.txt", vec!["pause"]),
        ("down_resume.txt", vec!["resume"]),
        ("down_unpair.txt", vec!["unpair", "someone"]),
    ] {
        check(name, &args, 1);
    }
}
```

```rust
#[cfg(test)]
mod compat {
    //! The control protocol is index-encoded by postcard. These tests pin the bytes of every variant
    //! that existed in v0.3.6, so a reordered or inserted variant fails here instead of breaking a
    //! running daemon or an older `klip`.
    use super::*;
    use crate::hlc::NodeId;

    fn req(r: &IpcRequest) -> Vec<u8> {
        serialize_ipc_msg(r).unwrap()
    }
    fn resp(r: &IpcResponse) -> Vec<u8> {
        serialize_ipc_msg(r).unwrap()
    }

    #[test]
    fn existing_request_variants_keep_their_wire_bytes() {
        assert_eq!(req(&IpcRequest::GetStatus), [0]);
        assert_eq!(req(&IpcRequest::PairStart { ttl_secs: 1, include_lan_addrs: false })[0], 1);
        assert_eq!(req(&IpcRequest::PairPoll), [2]);
        assert_eq!(req(&IpcRequest::PairDecide { accept: true }), [3, 1]);
        assert_eq!(req(&IpcRequest::PairCancel), [4]);
        assert_eq!(req(&IpcRequest::PairJoin { ticket: "k".into() }), [5, 1, b'k']);
        assert_eq!(req(&IpcRequest::Unpair { selector: "ab".into() }), [6, 2, b'a', b'b']);
        assert_eq!(req(&IpcRequest::Pause { duration_secs: 600 }), [7, 0xD8, 0x04]);
        assert_eq!(req(&IpcRequest::Resume), [8]);
        assert_eq!(req(&IpcRequest::GetHistory { limit: 10 }), [9, 10]);
        assert_eq!(req(&IpcRequest::SendClip { text: "hi".into() }), [10, 2, b'h', b'i']);
        assert_eq!(req(&IpcRequest::StopDaemon), [11]);
    }

    #[test]
    fn new_request_variants_are_appended_after_the_old_ones() {
        assert_eq!(req(&IpcRequest::Hello { client_protocol: 2 }), [12, 2]);
        assert_eq!(req(&IpcRequest::UnpairAll), [13]);
        assert_eq!(req(&IpcRequest::CopyToClipboard { id: [0; 16] })[0], 14);
        assert_eq!(req(&IpcRequest::ClearHistory), [15]);
    }

    #[test]
    fn existing_response_variants_keep_their_wire_bytes() {
        let status = DaemonStatusInfo {
            is_active: true,
            is_paused: false,
            paused_remaining_secs: 0,
            platform: String::new(),
            memory_rss_bytes: 0,
            node_id: NodeId::ZERO,
            relay_mode: String::new(),
            relay_state: String::new(),
            pairing_open: false,
            peers: vec![],
            total_synced_clips: 0,
            last_sync_time_secs_ago: None,
            last_clip_preview: None,
            last_clip_size_bytes: 0,
        };
        assert_eq!(resp(&IpcResponse::Status(status))[0], 0);
        assert_eq!(
            resp(&IpcResponse::PairTicket { ticket: String::new(), expires_in_secs: 0, host_fingerprint: String::new() })[0],
            1
        );
        assert_eq!(resp(&IpcResponse::PairPoll(PairPollInfo::default()))[0], 2);
        assert_eq!(
            resp(&IpcResponse::PairSuccess { peer_name: String::new(), peer_fingerprint: String::new() })[0],
            3
        );
        assert_eq!(resp(&IpcResponse::PairCancelled), [4]);
        assert_eq!(resp(&IpcResponse::Unpaired { name: String::new() })[0], 5);
        assert_eq!(resp(&IpcResponse::Paused { remaining_secs: 1 }), [6, 1]);
        assert_eq!(resp(&IpcResponse::Resumed), [7]);
        assert_eq!(resp(&IpcResponse::History(vec![])), [8, 0]);
        assert_eq!(
            resp(&IpcResponse::Sent { bytes: 0, delivered: vec![], failed: vec![] }),
            [9, 0, 0, 0]
        );
        assert_eq!(resp(&IpcResponse::DaemonStopping), [10]);
        assert_eq!(resp(&IpcResponse::Error("x".into())), [11, 1, b'x']);
    }

    #[test]
    fn new_response_variants_are_appended_after_the_old_ones() {
        let hello = HelloInfo {
            protocol: IPC_PROTOCOL_VERSION,
            daemon_version: "0.4.0".into(),
            capabilities: vec![CAP_UNPAIR_ALL.into()],
            config: ConfigInfo {
                device_name: "d".into(),
                relay: "n0".into(),
                max_history_items: 50,
                max_clip_bytes: 1000,
                lan_discovery: true,
            },
            max_request_bytes: max_request_bytes(1000),
        };
        assert_eq!(resp(&IpcResponse::Hello(hello.clone()))[0], 12);
        assert_eq!(resp(&IpcResponse::UnpairedAll { removed: vec![], failed: vec![] }), [13, 0, 0]);
        assert_eq!(resp(&IpcResponse::Copied), [14]);
        assert_eq!(resp(&IpcResponse::HistoryCleared), [15]);
        let bytes = serialize_ipc_msg(&IpcResponse::Hello(hello.clone())).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(deserialize_ipc_msg::<IpcResponse>(&bytes).unwrap(), IpcResponse::Hello(hello));
    }

    #[test]
    fn an_old_peer_decodes_a_new_request_as_an_error_not_a_panic() {
        // Index 12 is unknown to a v0.3.6 daemon; the decoder must report an error (which the daemon
        // turns into IpcResponse::Error). Simulated here with an index far beyond any variant.
        assert!(deserialize_ipc_msg::<IpcRequest>(&[200, 1, 2]).is_err());
    }

    #[test]
    fn request_cap_follows_the_clip_limit() {
        assert_eq!(max_request_bytes(1000), 1000 + 64 * 1024);
        assert_eq!(max_request_bytes(usize::MAX), usize::MAX);
    }
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement the additions**

In `crates/klip-proto/src/ipc.rs`, add above `pub enum IpcRequest`:

```rust
/// Version of the control protocol. 1 = before `Hello` existed (v0.3.6 and older); 2 adds `Hello`,
/// `UnpairAll`, `CopyToClipboard` and `ClearHistory`. Variants are only ever appended.
pub const IPC_PROTOCOL_VERSION: u32 = 2;

/// Room for the request's own framing and fields on top of the largest clip.
pub const IPC_REQUEST_OVERHEAD: usize = 64 * 1024;

/// Largest request a daemon with this clip limit accepts.
pub fn max_request_bytes(max_clip_bytes: usize) -> usize {
    max_clip_bytes.saturating_add(IPC_REQUEST_OVERHEAD)
}

pub const CAP_UNPAIR_ALL: &str = "unpair_all";
pub const CAP_COPY_TO_CLIPBOARD: &str = "copy_to_clipboard";
pub const CAP_CLEAR_HISTORY: &str = "clear_history";

/// The settings the running daemon loaded (what a restart would re-read from `config.json`).
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct ConfigInfo {
    pub device_name: String,
    /// `"n0"` or `"lan"`.
    pub relay: String,
    pub max_history_items: usize,
    pub max_clip_bytes: usize,
    pub lan_discovery: bool,
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct HelloInfo {
    pub protocol: u32,
    pub daemon_version: String,
    pub capabilities: Vec<String>,
    pub config: ConfigInfo,
    /// Largest request this daemon accepts (`max_request_bytes(config.max_clip_bytes)`).
    pub max_request_bytes: usize,
}
```

```rust
    /// First call of protocol 2: who is the daemon, what can it do, which limits apply.
    Hello {
        client_protocol: u32,
    },
    /// Remove every linked device.
    UnpairAll,
    /// Put a history item back on this device's clipboard (not re-broadcast).
    CopyToClipboard {
        id: [u8; 16],
    },
    /// Forget the clips kept in this device's history.
    ClearHistory,
```

### Task 3: History fixes and core operations (`unpair_all`, `copy_history_item`, `clear_history`)

**Files:**
- Modify: `crates/klip-core/src/history.rs`
- Modify: `crates/klip-core/src/sync.rs`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `ClipContentType` from `klip_proto::clip`.
- Produces:
  - `pub fn sniff_content_type(content: &[u8]) -> ClipContentType` (history.rs)
  - `HistoryBuffer::get_by_id(&self, id: [u8; 16]) -> Option<HistoryItem>` and `HistoryBuffer::clear(&self)`
  - `SyncCoordinator::unpair_all(&self) -> (Vec<String>, Vec<(String, String)>)` (async)
  - `SyncCoordinator::copy_history_item(&self, id: [u8; 16]) -> Result<(), String>` (async)
  - `SyncCoordinator::clear_history(&self)`

While planning I found a real bug: `history.rs` treats any text that starts with `BM` as a bitmap image (`content.starts_with(b"BM")`), so a clip such as `BMW M3` is listed as `[Image: Bitmap (6 B)]`. The history view of the TUI would show it wrongly, and `CopyToClipboard` must not mistake text for an image.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

```rust
    fn text_that_starts_with_bm_is_text_not_a_bitmap() {
        let buf = HistoryBuffer::new(3);
        let node = NodeId::new([1u8; 32]);
        buf.push([1; 16], node, "n".into(), b"BMW M3 is a fast car indeed".to_vec(), false);
        let items = buf.get_recent(1);
        assert_eq!(items[0].content_preview, "BMW M3 is a fast car indeed");
        assert_eq!(sniff_content_type(b"BMW M3 is a fast car indeed"), klip_proto::clip::ClipContentType::PlainTextUtf8);

        bmp.extend_from_slice(&[0, 0, 0, 0]); // reserved
        bmp.extend_from_slice(&[54, 0, 0, 0]); // pixel data offset
        bmp.extend_from_slice(&[40, 0, 0, 0]); // DIB header size = BITMAPINFOHEADER
        bmp.extend_from_slice(&[0u8; 36]);
        let buf = HistoryBuffer::new(3);
        buf.push([2; 16], NodeId::new([1u8; 32]), "n".into(), bmp, false);

        let node = NodeId::new([1u8; 32]);
        buf.push([7; 16], node, "n".into(), b"seven".to_vec(), false);
        buf.push([8; 16], node, "n".into(), b"eight".to_vec(), false);
        assert_eq!(buf.get_by_id([7; 16]).unwrap().full_content, b"seven");
        assert!(buf.get_by_id([9; 16]).is_none());
        buf.clear();

        assert_eq!(sniff_content_type(b"RIFF\x00\x00\x00\x00WEBPVP8 "), T::ImageWebP);
        assert_eq!(sniff_content_type(b"RIFF is also a word"), T::PlainTextUtf8);
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement in `history.rs`**

Add at the top: `use klip_proto::clip::ClipContentType;`. Add above `impl HistoryBuffer`:

```rust
/// What a stored clip is, judged from its bytes (the history keeps no content type). Text is the
/// default; images are recognised only by their real magic bytes.
pub fn sniff_content_type(content: &[u8]) -> ClipContentType {
    if content.starts_with(b"\x89PNG\r\n\x1a\n") {
        ClipContentType::ImagePng
    } else if content.len() >= 12 && &content[0..4] == b"RIFF" && &content[8..12] == b"WEBP" {
        ClipContentType::ImageWebP
    } else {
        ClipContentType::PlainTextUtf8
    }
}

/// A Windows bitmap: `BM`, then a 14-byte file header, then a DIB header whose size field (u32 LE at
/// offset 14) is one of the known header sizes. Text that merely starts with "BM" does not match.
fn is_bitmap(content: &[u8]) -> bool {
    content.len() >= 26
        && content.starts_with(b"BM")
        && matches!(
            u32::from_le_bytes([content[14], content[15], content[16], content[17]]),
            12 | 40 | 52 | 56 | 64 | 108 | 124
        )
}
```

In `push`, replace `} else if content.starts_with(b"BM") {` with `} else if is_bitmap(&content) {`. Add to `impl HistoryBuffer`:

```rust
    pub fn get_by_id(&self, id: [u8; 16]) -> Option<HistoryItem> {
        let lock = self.items.lock().unwrap();
        lock.iter().find(|i| i.id == id).cloned()
    }

    /// Forget everything kept in history (local only; nothing is sent to other devices).
    pub fn clear(&self) {
        self.items.lock().unwrap().clear();
    }
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Add the three coordinator operations**

In `crates/klip-core/src/sync.rs`, add inside `impl SyncCoordinator` right after the closing brace of `pub async fn unpair`:

```rust
    /// Remove every linked device. Continues after a failure and reports both lists
    /// (`removed` names, and `(name, reason)` for each failure).
    pub async fn unpair_all(&self) -> (Vec<String>, Vec<(String, String)>) {
        let mut removed = Vec::new();
        let mut failed = Vec::new();
        for peer in self.net.peers() {
            let selector = node_of(&peer.id).fingerprint();
            match self.unpair(&selector).await {
                Ok(name) => removed.push(name),
                Err(reason) => failed.push((peer.name, reason)),
            }
        }
        (removed, failed)
    }

    /// Put a history item back on this device's clipboard. It goes through the same driver call as a
    /// received clip, which records it in the echo shield, so it is not sent out again as a new copy.
    pub async fn copy_history_item(&self, id: [u8; 16]) -> Result<(), String> {
        let item = self
            .history
            .get_by_id(id)
            .ok_or_else(|| "that clip is no longer in the history".to_string())?;
        if item.full_content.is_empty() {
            return Err("that clip was too large to keep in the history; copy it again on the device it came from".to_string());
        }
        let content_type = crate::history::sniff_content_type(&item.full_content);
        let driver = Arc::clone(&self.driver);
        let sensitive = item.is_sensitive;
        let data = item.full_content;
        tokio::task::spawn_blocking(move || {
            driver
                .write_clip_flagged(&content_type, &data, sensitive)
                .map_err(|e| e.to_string())
        })
        .await
        .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?
    }

    pub fn clear_history(&self) {
        self.history.clear();
    }
```

```rust
fn hello_of(r: IpcResponse) -> klip_proto::ipc::HelloInfo {
    match r {
        IpcResponse::Hello(h) => h,
        other => panic!("expected Hello, got {other:?}"),
    }
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn hello_reports_version_capabilities_config_and_the_request_cap() {
    let a = start_daemon_with("Alpha", fresh_dir("h1"), false, |c| c.max_clip_bytes = 5000).await;
    let h = hello_of(a.ipc(IpcRequest::Hello { client_protocol: 2 }).await);
    assert_eq!(h.protocol, klip_proto::ipc::IPC_PROTOCOL_VERSION);
    assert_eq!(h.daemon_version, env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION"));
    for cap in ["unpair_all", "copy_to_clipboard", "clear_history"] {
        assert!(h.capabilities.iter().any(|c| c == cap), "missing capability {cap}");
    }
    assert_eq!(h.config.device_name, "Alpha");
    assert_eq!(h.config.relay, "lan");
    assert_eq!(h.config.max_clip_bytes, 5000);
    assert_eq!(h.max_request_bytes, 5000 + 64 * 1024);
    a.stop().await;
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn unpair_all_removes_every_device_and_reports_the_names() {
    let a = start_daemon("Alpha", fresh_dir("u1a"), false).await;
    let b = start_daemon("Beta", fresh_dir("u1b"), false).await;
    let c = start_daemon("Gamma", fresh_dir("u1c"), false).await;
    assert!(matches!(pair(&a, &b, true).await, IpcResponse::PairSuccess { .. }));
    assert!(matches!(pair(&a, &c, true).await, IpcResponse::PairSuccess { .. }));
    match a.ipc(IpcRequest::GetStatus).await {
        IpcResponse::Status(s) => assert_eq!(s.peers.len(), 2),
        other => panic!("{other:?}"),
    }
    match a.ipc(IpcRequest::UnpairAll).await {
        IpcResponse::UnpairedAll { mut removed, failed } => {
            removed.sort();
            assert_eq!(removed, ["Beta", "Gamma"]);
            assert!(failed.is_empty(), "{failed:?}");
        }
        other => panic!("{other:?}"),
    }
    match a.ipc(IpcRequest::GetStatus).await {
        IpcResponse::Status(s) => assert!(s.peers.is_empty()),
        other => panic!("{other:?}"),
    }
    // Nothing left to remove: still a clean answer.
    assert!(matches!(
        a.ipc(IpcRequest::UnpairAll).await,
        IpcResponse::UnpairedAll { removed, failed } if removed.is_empty() && failed.is_empty()
    ));
    a.stop().await;
    b.stop().await;
    c.stop().await;
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn copy_to_clipboard_restores_an_item_without_sending_it_out_again() {
    let a = start_daemon("Alpha", fresh_dir("c1a"), false).await;
    let b = start_daemon("Beta", fresh_dir("c1b"), false).await;
    assert!(matches!(pair(&a, &b, true).await, IpcResponse::PairSuccess { .. }));

    a.clipboard.user_copy("first");
    assert!(wait_until(10, || b.clipboard.current_text().as_deref() == Some("first")).await);
    b.clipboard.user_copy("second");
    assert!(wait_until(10, || a.clipboard.current_text().as_deref() == Some("second")).await);

    let id = match b.ipc(IpcRequest::GetHistory { limit: 10 }).await {
        IpcResponse::History(items) => items
            .iter()
            .find(|i| i.full_content == b"first")
            .expect("'first' is in Beta's history")
            .id,
        other => panic!("{other:?}"),
    };
    assert!(matches!(b.ipc(IpcRequest::CopyToClipboard { id }).await, IpcResponse::Copied));
    assert_eq!(b.clipboard.current_text().as_deref(), Some("first"));
    // If the copy had been broadcast as a new local copy, Alpha would now show "first".
    tokio::time::sleep(Duration::from_millis(1200)).await;
    assert_eq!(a.clipboard.current_text().as_deref(), Some("second"));

    // An unknown id is a plain error.
    match b.ipc(IpcRequest::CopyToClipboard { id: [9; 16] }).await {
        IpcResponse::Error(e) => assert!(e.contains("no longer in the history"), "{e}"),
        other => panic!("{other:?}"),
    }
    a.stop().await;
    b.stop().await;
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn clear_history_forgets_the_clips() {
    let a = start_daemon("Alpha", fresh_dir("ch1"), false).await;
    let b = start_daemon("Beta", fresh_dir("ch2"), false).await;
    assert!(matches!(pair(&a, &b, true).await, IpcResponse::PairSuccess { .. }));
    a.clipboard.user_copy("remember me");
    assert!(wait_until(10, || b.clipboard.current_text().as_deref() == Some("remember me")).await);
    assert!(matches!(
        b.ipc(IpcRequest::GetHistory { limit: 10 }).await,
        IpcResponse::History(items) if !items.is_empty()
    ));
    assert!(matches!(b.ipc(IpcRequest::ClearHistory).await, IpcResponse::HistoryCleared));
    assert!(matches!(
        b.ipc(IpcRequest::GetHistory { limit: 10 }).await,
        IpcResponse::History(items) if items.is_empty()
    ));
    a.stop().await;
    b.stop().await;
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn a_request_over_the_cap_gets_a_clear_error() {
    let a = start_daemon_with("Alpha", fresh_dir("cap1"), false, |c| c.max_clip_bytes = 1000).await;
    let mut s = UnixStream::connect(a.sock()).await.unwrap();
    // Announce 1 MB (cap is 1000 + 64 KiB) and send nothing else: the answer must come anyway.
    s.write_all(&1_000_000u32.to_be_bytes()).await.unwrap();
    let mut len = [0u8; 4];
    s.read_exact(&mut len).await.unwrap();
    let mut buf = vec![0u8; u32::from_be_bytes(len) as usize];
    s.read_exact(&mut buf).await.unwrap();
    match deserialize_ipc_msg::<IpcResponse>(&buf).unwrap() {
        IpcResponse::Error(e) => assert!(e.contains("request too large") && e.contains("66536"), "{e}"),
        other => panic!("{other:?}"),
    }
    // The daemon is still fine.
    assert!(matches!(a.ipc(IpcRequest::GetStatus).await, IpcResponse::Status(_)));
    a.stop().await;
}

fn vm_size_kib() -> u64 {
    std::fs::read_to_string("/proc/self/status")
        .unwrap()
        .lines()
        .find(|l| l.starts_with("VmSize:"))
        .and_then(|l| l.split_whitespace().nth(1))
        .and_then(|v| v.parse().ok())
        .unwrap()
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn a_client_that_announces_a_huge_length_and_sends_nothing_costs_no_memory() {
    // Cap = 256 MiB + 64 KiB, so a 200 MB announcement is within the cap and used to be allocated up front.
    let a = start_daemon_with("Alpha", fresh_dir("mem1"), false, |c| c.max_clip_bytes = 256 * 1024 * 1024).await;
    let before = vm_size_kib();
    let mut held = Vec::new();
    for _ in 0..8 {
        let mut s = UnixStream::connect(a.sock()).await.unwrap();
        s.write_all(&200_000_000u32.to_be_bytes()).await.unwrap();
        held.push(s); // keep the connections open, send no body
    }
    tokio::time::sleep(Duration::from_millis(300)).await;
    let grown_mib = vm_size_kib().saturating_sub(before) / 1024;
    assert!(grown_mib < 400, "8 stalled requests grew the process by {grown_mib} MiB of address space");
    drop(held);
    a.stop().await;
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement in `ipc_server.rs`**

Replace the line `pub const MAX_IPC_REQUEST_BYTES: usize = 8 * 1024 * 1024;` and its doc comment with nothing (delete both). Add imports: `use klip_core::RelayMode;` and extend the proto import:

```rust
use klip_proto::ipc::{
    deserialize_ipc_msg, max_request_bytes, serialize_ipc_msg, ConfigInfo, HelloInfo, IpcRequest,
    IpcResponse, CAP_CLEAR_HISTORY, CAP_COPY_TO_CLIPBOARD, CAP_UNPAIR_ALL, IPC_PROTOCOL_VERSION,
};
```

Replace `handle_client`'s read line and `read_request`:

```rust
    #[cfg(unix)]
    async fn handle_client(
        mut stream: UnixStream,
        sync: Arc<SyncCoordinator>,
        shutdown: Arc<Shutdown>,
    ) {
        let cap = max_request_bytes(sync.config.max_clip_bytes);
        let request = match timeout(IPC_IO_TIMEOUT, Self::read_request(&mut stream, cap)).await {
            Ok(Ok(req)) => req,
            Ok(Err(msg)) => {
                let _ = Self::send_response(&mut stream, &IpcResponse::Error(msg)).await;
                return;
            }
            Err(_) => return,
        };
        let response = Self::dispatch(request, &sync, &shutdown).await;
        let _ = timeout(IPC_IO_TIMEOUT, Self::send_response(&mut stream, &response)).await;
    }

    #[cfg(unix)]
    async fn read_request(stream: &mut UnixStream, cap: usize) -> Result<IpcRequest, String> {
        let mut len_buf = [0u8; 4];
        stream
            .read_exact(&mut len_buf)
            .await
            .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
        let len = u32::from_be_bytes(len_buf) as usize;
        if len > cap {
            return Err(format!(
                "request too large ({len} bytes; this daemon accepts at most {cap})"
            ));
        }
        // Grow the buffer as bytes arrive. A client that announces a huge length and then sends
        // nothing must not make the daemon reserve that much memory.
        let mut buf = Vec::new();
        let got = (&mut *stream)
            .take(len as u64)
            .read_to_end(&mut buf)
            .await
            .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
        if got != len {
            return Err("request ended before its announced length".to_string());
        }
        deserialize_ipc_msg(&buf)
    }
```

```rust
                Ok(()) => IpcResponse::Copied,
```

```rust
//! A stub that behaves like a v0.3.6 daemon: it does not know protocol 2, caps requests at 8 MiB.
#![cfg(unix)]
use klip_cli::client::{ClientError, DaemonClient, OLD_DAEMON_REQUEST_CAP};
use klip_cli::ops::{request_cap, unpair_all_compat};
use klip_proto::hlc::NodeId;
use klip_proto::ipc::{
    deserialize_ipc_msg, serialize_ipc_msg, DaemonStatusInfo, IpcRequest, IpcResponse,
    PeerStatusInfo,
};
use std::path::PathBuf;
use std::sync::{Arc, Mutex};
use tokio::io::{AsyncReadExt, AsyncWriteExt};
use tokio::net::UnixListener;

fn status_with(names: &[&str]) -> DaemonStatusInfo {
    DaemonStatusInfo {
        is_active: true,
        is_paused: false,
        paused_remaining_secs: 0,
        platform: "stub".into(),
        memory_rss_bytes: 0,
        node_id: NodeId::ZERO,
        relay_mode: String::new(),
        relay_state: String::new(),
        pairing_open: false,
        peers: names
            .iter()
            .enumerate()
            .map(|(i, n)| PeerStatusInfo {
                node_id: NodeId::new([i as u8 + 1; 32]),
                name: n.to_string(),
                is_online: false,
                last_error: None,
            })
            .collect(),
        total_synced_clips: 0,
        last_sync_time_secs_ago: None,
        last_clip_preview: None,
        last_clip_size_bytes: 0,
    }
}

/// Serves requests like an old daemon: unknown variants are decode errors answered with Error.
fn spawn_old_daemon(dir: &PathBuf, unpaired: Arc<Mutex<Vec<String>>>) {
    let sock = dir.join("old.sock");
    let listener = UnixListener::bind(&sock).unwrap();
    tokio::spawn(async move {
        loop {
            let (mut s, _) = listener.accept().await.unwrap();
            let unpaired = Arc::clone(&unpaired);
            tokio::spawn(async move {
                let mut len = [0u8; 4];
                if s.read_exact(&mut len).await.is_err() {
                    return;
                }
                let mut buf = vec![0u8; u32::from_be_bytes(len) as usize];
                if s.read_exact(&mut buf).await.is_err() {
                    return;
                }
                let reply = match deserialize_ipc_msg::<IpcRequest>(&buf) {
                    // The old daemon's enum ends at StopDaemon (index 11): 12.. are decode errors.
                    Ok(IpcRequest::Hello { .. })
                    | Ok(IpcRequest::UnpairAll)
                    | Ok(IpcRequest::CopyToClipboard { .. })
                    | Ok(IpcRequest::ClearHistory) => {
                        IpcResponse::Error("Found a variant that does not exist".into())
                    }
                    Ok(IpcRequest::GetStatus) => IpcResponse::Status(status_with(&["Beta", "Beta"])),
                    Ok(IpcRequest::Unpair { selector }) => {
                        unpaired.lock().unwrap().push(selector.clone());
                        IpcResponse::Unpaired { name: "Beta".into() }
                    }
                    _ => IpcResponse::Error("unsupported in the stub".into()),
                };
                let out = serialize_ipc_msg(&reply).unwrap();
                let _ = s.write_all(&(out.len() as u32).to_be_bytes()).await;
                let _ = s.write_all(&out).await;
            });
        }
    });
}

fn temp(tag: &str) -> PathBuf {
    let d = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("klip-old-{}-{tag}", std::process::id()));
    let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&d);
    std::fs::create_dir_all(&d).unwrap();
    d
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn an_old_daemon_gets_the_8_mib_cap_and_oversize_text_is_refused_before_writing() {
    let dir = temp("cap");
    spawn_old_daemon(&dir, Arc::default());
    let client = DaemonClient::new(dir.join("old.sock"));
    assert_eq!(request_cap(&client).await, OLD_DAEMON_REQUEST_CAP);
    let big = IpcRequest::SendClip { text: "a".repeat(9_000_000) };
    match client.request(&big, OLD_DAEMON_REQUEST_CAP).await {
        Err(ClientError::TooLarge { size, max }) => {
            assert!(size > 9_000_000);
            assert_eq!(max, OLD_DAEMON_REQUEST_CAP);
        }
        other => panic!("expected TooLarge, got {other:?}"),
    }
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn unpair_all_falls_back_to_one_call_per_device_on_an_old_daemon() {
    let dir = temp("unpair");
    let log = Arc::new(Mutex::new(Vec::new()));
    spawn_old_daemon(&dir, Arc::clone(&log));
    let client = DaemonClient::new(dir.join("old.sock"));
    let (removed, failed) = unpair_all_compat(&client).await.unwrap();
    assert_eq!(removed, ["Beta", "Beta"]);
    assert!(failed.is_empty());
    assert_eq!(log.lock().unwrap().len(), 2, "one Unpair per device");
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn a_missing_socket_is_reported_as_the_daemon_being_down() {
    let client = DaemonClient::new(temp("down").join("none.sock"));
    match client.request(&IpcRequest::GetStatus, OLD_DAEMON_REQUEST_CAP).await {
        Err(ClientError::Down(m)) => assert!(m.contains("not running"), "{m}"),
        other => panic!("{other:?}"),
    }
}
```

Create `crates/klip-cli/tests/cli_daemon.rs`:

```rust
//! The real `klip` binary against an isolated daemon (own config dir, in-memory clipboard).
#![cfg(unix)]
use std::io::Write;
use std::path::PathBuf;
use std::process::{Command, Output, Stdio};

struct Isolated {
    dir: PathBuf,
}

impl Isolated {
    fn start(tag: &str, config: &str) -> Self {
        let dir = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("klip-cli-{}-{tag}", std::process::id()));
        let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
        std::fs::create_dir_all(&dir).unwrap();
        std::fs::write(dir.join("config.json"), config).unwrap();
        let me = Self { dir };
        let out = me.run(&["start"], None);
        assert!(out.status.success(), "start failed: {}", text(&out));
        me
    }

    fn run(&self, args: &[&str], stdin: Option<&[u8]>) -> Output {
        let mut cmd = Command::new(env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_klip"));
        cmd.args(args)
            .env("KLIP_CONFIG_DIR", &self.dir)
            .env("KLIP_CLIPBOARD", "memory")
            .stdout(Stdio::piped())
            .stderr(Stdio::piped());
        match stdin {
            Some(bytes) => {
                cmd.stdin(Stdio::piped());
                let mut child = cmd.spawn().unwrap();
                let mut sin = child.stdin.take().unwrap();
                let data = bytes.to_vec();
                let writer = std::thread::spawn(move || {
                    let _ = sin.write_all(&data); // the CLI may stop reading early on an error
                });
                let out = child.wait_with_output().unwrap();
                let _ = writer.join();
                out
            }
            None => {
                cmd.stdin(Stdio::null());
                cmd.output().unwrap()
            }
        }
    }
}

impl Drop for Isolated {
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        let _ = self.run(&["stop"], None);
        let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&self.dir);
    }
}

fn text(o: &Output) -> String {
    format!("{}{}", String::from_utf8_lossy(&o.stdout), String::from_utf8_lossy(&o.stderr))
}

const LAN_ONLY: &str = r#"{"relay":"lan","lan_discovery":false}"#;

#[test]
fn history_clear_forgets_the_clips() {
    let d = Isolated::start("hc", LAN_ONLY);
    assert!(d.run(&["send", "remember-this-text"], None).status.success());
    assert!(text(&d.run(&["history"], None)).contains("remember-this-text"));
    let cleared = d.run(&["history", "clear", "--yes"], None);
    assert!(cleared.status.success(), "{}", text(&cleared));
    assert!(text(&d.run(&["history"], None)).contains("No clips"), "history should be empty");
}

#[test]
fn unpair_all_with_no_devices_says_so() {
    let d = Isolated::start("ua", LAN_ONLY);
    let out = d.run(&["unpair", "--all", "--yes"], None);
    assert!(out.status.success(), "{}", text(&out));
    assert!(text(&out).contains("No linked devices"), "{}", text(&out));
}

#[test]
fn unpair_needs_a_device_or_all_and_not_both() {
    let d = Isolated::start("ub", LAN_ONLY);
    assert!(!d.run(&["unpair"], None).status.success());
    assert!(!d.run(&["unpair", "x", "--all"], None).status.success());
}

#[test]
fn a_9_mb_text_through_stdin_gets_a_normal_answer_not_a_broken_pipe() {
    let d = Isolated::start("big", LAN_ONLY);
    let text_9mb = vec![b'a'; 9_000_000];
    let out = d.run(&["send", "-"], Some(&text_9mb));
    let all = text(&out);
    assert!(!all.contains("Broken pipe"), "{all}");
    assert!(out.status.success(), "{all}");
    assert!(all.contains("No linked devices"), "{all}");
}

#[test]
fn text_over_the_daemons_cap_is_refused_before_it_is_written() {
    let d = Isolated::start("cap", r#"{"relay":"lan","lan_discovery":false,"max_clip_bytes":1000}"#);
    let out = d.run(&["send", "-"], Some(&vec![b'b'; 100_000]));
    let all = text(&out);
    assert!(!out.status.success(), "{all}");
    assert!(all.contains("at most 66536"), "{all}");
    assert!(!all.contains("Broken pipe"), "{all}");
}
```

```rust
use klip_daemon::config::get_ipc_socket_path;
use klip_proto::ipc::{deserialize_ipc_msg, serialize_ipc_msg, IpcRequest, IpcResponse};
use std::path::PathBuf;
use std::time::Duration;
use tokio::io::{AsyncReadExt, AsyncWriteExt};
#[cfg(unix)]
use tokio::net::UnixStream;

/// Most requests are answered in milliseconds; a hung daemon must not hang the CLI forever.
const DEFAULT_TIMEOUT: Duration = Duration::from_secs(15);
/// Joining a device waits for the other side's human to approve it.
const PAIR_JOIN_TIMEOUT: Duration = Duration::from_secs(120);
/// What daemons that predate the `Hello` call accept in one request.
pub const OLD_DAEMON_REQUEST_CAP: usize = 8 * 1024 * 1024;
/// Answers bigger than this are refused as implausible (history holds at most ~8 MiB of content).
const MAX_RESPONSE_BYTES: usize = 64 * 1024 * 1024;

#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum ClientError {
    /// No socket, or nobody answers on it. The text is the message the CLI has always shown.
    Down(String),
    Timeout(u64),
    TooLarge { size: usize, max: usize },
    Io(String),
}

impl std::fmt::Display for ClientError {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
        match self {
            Self::Down(m) | Self::Io(m) => write!(f, "{m}"),
            Self::Timeout(s) => write!(
                f,
                "Klip did not answer within {s} s; it may be stuck. Try `klip stop`, or stop the process."
            ),
            Self::TooLarge { size, max } => write!(
                f,
                "the text is {size} bytes but one request can carry at most {max} bytes"
            ),
        }
    }
}

impl std::error::Error for ClientError {}

pub struct DaemonClient {
    sock: PathBuf,
}

/// A big clip is delivered to every device before the daemon answers `klip send`: allow time for it.
fn timeout_for(req: &IpcRequest) -> Duration {
    match req {
        IpcRequest::PairJoin { .. } => PAIR_JOIN_TIMEOUT,
        IpcRequest::SendClip { text } => {
            DEFAULT_TIMEOUT + Duration::from_secs((text.len() / (256 * 1024)) as u64)
        }
        _ => DEFAULT_TIMEOUT,
    }
}

impl DaemonClient {
    pub fn new(sock: impl Into<PathBuf>) -> Self {
        Self { sock: sock.into() }
    }

    pub fn for_current_user() -> Self {
        Self::new(get_ipc_socket_path())
    }

    /// Existing callers: the current user's daemon, the 8 MiB request cap of older daemons.
    pub async fn send_request(req: &IpcRequest) -> Result<IpcResponse, String> {
        Self::for_current_user()
            .request(req, OLD_DAEMON_REQUEST_CAP)
            .await
            .map_err(|e| e.to_string())
    }

    /// Send one request. `max_request_bytes` is what the daemon accepts (see `ops::request_cap`);
    /// a bigger request is refused here, before a single byte is written.
    pub async fn request(
        &self,
        req: &IpcRequest,
        max_request_bytes: usize,
    ) -> Result<IpcResponse, ClientError> {
        let limit = timeout_for(req);
        tokio::time::timeout(limit, self.exchange(req, max_request_bytes))
            .await
            .map_err(|_| ClientError::Timeout(limit.as_secs()))?
    }

    #[cfg(unix)]
    async fn exchange(
        &self,
        req: &IpcRequest,
        max_request_bytes: usize,
    ) -> Result<IpcResponse, ClientError> {
        if !self.sock.exists() {
            return Err(ClientError::Down(
                "Klip daemon is not running. Launch it with `klip start`.".to_string(),
            ));
        }
        let mut stream = UnixStream::connect(&self.sock).await.map_err(|_| {
            ClientError::Down(
                "Klip daemon is not running or not responding. Launch it with `klip start`."
                    .to_string(),
            )
        })?;

        let serialized = serialize_ipc_msg(req).map_err(ClientError::Io)?;
        if serialized.len() > max_request_bytes {
            return Err(ClientError::TooLarge {
                size: serialized.len(),
                max: max_request_bytes,
            });
        }
        let written = async {
            stream
                .write_all(&(serialized.len() as u32).to_be_bytes())
                .await?;
            stream.write_all(&serialized).await?;
            stream.flush().await
        }
        .await;
        if let Err(e) = written {
            // The daemon may have answered (for example "request too large") before it closed the
            // connection: that answer is more useful than "Broken pipe".
            return match Self::read_response(&mut stream).await {
                Ok(resp) => Ok(resp),
                Err(_) => Err(ClientError::Io(e.to_string())),
            };
        }
        Self::read_response(&mut stream).await
    }

    #[cfg(unix)]
    async fn read_response(stream: &mut UnixStream) -> Result<IpcResponse, ClientError> {
        let mut resp_len_buf = [0u8; 4];
        stream
            .read_exact(&mut resp_len_buf)
            .await
            .map_err(|e| ClientError::Io(format!("Failed to read response length: {e}")))?;
        let resp_len = u32::from_be_bytes(resp_len_buf) as usize;
        if resp_len > MAX_RESPONSE_BYTES {
            return Err(ClientError::Io(
                "daemon sent an implausible response size".to_string(),
            ));
        }
        let mut resp_buf = vec![0u8; resp_len];
        stream
            .read_exact(&mut resp_buf)
            .await
            .map_err(|e| ClientError::Io(format!("Failed to read response data: {e}")))?;
        deserialize_ipc_msg(&resp_buf).map_err(ClientError::Io)
    }

    #[cfg(not(unix))]
    async fn exchange(
        &self,
        _req: &IpcRequest,
        _max_request_bytes: usize,
    ) -> Result<IpcResponse, ClientError> {
        Err(ClientError::Io("Unix socket not supported on this platform".to_string()))
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn big_sends_get_more_time_and_other_requests_keep_the_old_limits() {
        assert_eq!(timeout_for(&IpcRequest::GetStatus), DEFAULT_TIMEOUT);
        assert_eq!(timeout_for(&IpcRequest::PairJoin { ticket: String::new() }), PAIR_JOIN_TIMEOUT);
        let big = IpcRequest::SendClip { text: "x".repeat(30 * 1024 * 1024) };
        assert_eq!(timeout_for(&big), DEFAULT_TIMEOUT + Duration::from_secs(120));
    }

    #[test]
    fn messages_for_the_daemon_being_down_are_unchanged() {
        assert_eq!(
            ClientError::Timeout(15).to_string(),
            "Klip did not answer within 15 s; it may be stuck. Try `klip stop`, or stop the process."
        );
    }
}
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Create `ops.rs` and register modules**

`crates/klip-cli/src/ops.rs`:

```rust
//! Operations built from several control calls, shared by the subcommands and the TUI.
use crate::client::{ClientError, DaemonClient, OLD_DAEMON_REQUEST_CAP};
use klip_proto::ipc::{IpcRequest, IpcResponse, IPC_PROTOCOL_VERSION};

/// How many bytes one request may carry: asked from the daemon (protocol 2), 8 MiB for older daemons.
pub async fn request_cap(client: &DaemonClient) -> usize {
    match client
        .request(
            &IpcRequest::Hello { client_protocol: IPC_PROTOCOL_VERSION },
            OLD_DAEMON_REQUEST_CAP,
        )
        .await
    {
        Ok(IpcResponse::Hello(h)) => h.max_request_bytes,
        _ => OLD_DAEMON_REQUEST_CAP,
    }
}

/// Remove every linked device: one `UnpairAll` call, or, for a daemon that does not know it, one
/// `Unpair` per device. Returns `(removed names, (name, reason) failures)`.
pub async fn unpair_all_compat(
    client: &DaemonClient,
) -> Result<(Vec<String>, Vec<(String, String)>), ClientError> {
    if let Ok(IpcResponse::UnpairedAll { removed, failed }) = client
        .request(&IpcRequest::UnpairAll, OLD_DAEMON_REQUEST_CAP)
        .await
    {
        return Ok((removed, failed));
    }
    let peers = match client
        .request(&IpcRequest::GetStatus, OLD_DAEMON_REQUEST_CAP)
        .await?
    {
        IpcResponse::Status(s) => s.peers,
        IpcResponse::Error(e) => return Err(ClientError::Io(e)),
        other => return Err(ClientError::Io(format!("unexpected reply: {other:?}"))),
    };
    let mut removed = Vec::new();
    let mut failed = Vec::new();
    for peer in peers {
        let selector = peer.node_id.fingerprint();
        match client
            .request(&IpcRequest::Unpair { selector }, OLD_DAEMON_REQUEST_CAP)
            .await?
        {
            IpcResponse::Unpaired { name } => removed.push(name),
            IpcResponse::Error(e) => failed.push((peer.name, e)),
            other => failed.push((peer.name, format!("unexpected reply: {other:?}"))),
        }
    }
    Ok((removed, failed))
}
```

```rust
            let text = if text == "-" {
                use std::io::Read;
                if std::io::stdin().is_terminal() {
                    eprintln!("Reading the text from the terminal; finish with Ctrl-D.");
                }
                let mut buf = String::new();
                std::io::stdin()
                    .read_to_string(&mut buf)
                    .map_err(|e| format!("cannot read standard input as text: {e}"))?;
                buf
            } else {
                text
            };
            let client = DaemonClient::for_current_user();
            let cap = request_cap(&client).await;
            let reply = client
                .request(&IpcRequest::SendClip { text }, cap)
                .await
                .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
            match reply {
                IpcResponse::Sent {
                    bytes,
                    delivered,
                    failed,
                } => {
                    if delivered.is_empty() && failed.is_empty() {
                        println!(
                            "{} No linked devices. Run `klip pair` first.",
                            "!".yellow().bold()
                        );
                    }
                    for name in &delivered {
                        println!("{} Sent {bytes} B to {}", "✓".green().bold(), name.bold());
                    }
                    for (name, why) in &failed {
                        println!(
                            "{} {} did not receive it: {}",
                            "✗".red().bold(),
                            name.bold(),
                            why
                        );
                    }
                    if delivered.is_empty() && !failed.is_empty() {
                        return Err("could not reach any linked device".into());
                    }
                }
                IpcResponse::Error(e) => return Err(e.into()),
                other => return Err(format!("unexpected reply: {other:?}").into()),
            }
        }
```
The printed lines are the previous ones unchanged; only the way the text is read and the request is sent differs.

Add the two helper functions after `confirm`:

```rust
async fn unpair_all(yes: bool) -> AnyResult<()> {
    let peers = match rpc(IpcRequest::GetStatus).await? {
        IpcResponse::Status(s) => s.peers,
        other => return Err(format!("unexpected reply: {other:?}").into()),
    };
    if peers.is_empty() {
        println!("{}", "No linked devices.".yellow());
        return Ok(());
    }
    if !yes {
        let names = peers.iter().map(|p| p.name.as_str()).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
        let prompt = format!("Remove all {} linked devices ({names})? [y/N] ", peers.len());
        if !confirm(&prompt).await {
            println!("{}", "Cancelled.".yellow());
            return Ok(());
        }
    }
    let (removed, failed) = unpair_all_compat(&DaemonClient::for_current_user())
        .await
        .map_err(|e: ClientError| e.to_string())?;
    for name in &removed {
        println!("{} Removed {}", "✓".green().bold(), name.bold());
    }
    for (name, why) in &failed {
        eprintln!("{} could not remove {}: {why}", "error:".red().bold(), name.bold());
    }
    if failed.is_empty() {
        Ok(())
    } else {
        Err(format!("{} device(s) could not be removed", failed.len()).into())
    }
}

async fn history_clear(yes: bool) -> AnyResult<()> {
    if !yes && !confirm("Forget all clips kept in the history of this device? [y/N] ").await {
        println!("{}", "Cancelled.".yellow());
        return Ok(());
    }
    match DaemonClient::send_request(&IpcRequest::ClearHistory).await {
        Ok(IpcResponse::HistoryCleared) => {
            println!("{} History cleared.", "✓".green().bold());
            Ok(())
        }
        Ok(IpcResponse::Error(e)) | Err(e) => Err(format!(
            "could not clear the history ({e}). If the running daemon is older than this klip, restart it: klip stop && klip start"
        )
        .into()),
        Ok(other) => Err(format!("unexpected reply: {other:?}").into()),
    }
}
```

```rust
#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    fn no_control(s: &str) -> bool {
        s.chars().all(|c| c == '\n' || !c.is_control())
    }

    #[test]
    fn escape_sequences_never_survive() {
        let hostile = "ok \x1b[31mred\x1b[0m \x1b]0;pwned\x07 \u{9b}31m \x08\x7f end";
        let out = clean(hostile);
        assert!(no_control(&out), "{out:?}");
        for needle in ['␛', '␇', '␡'] {
            assert!(out.contains(needle), "{needle} missing in {out:?}");
        }
        assert!(out.contains("⟦U+009B⟧"), "{out:?}");
        assert!(out.starts_with("ok "));
    }

    #[test]
    fn bidi_tag_and_invisible_characters_are_made_visible() {
        let out = clean("abc\u{202E}def\u{2066}x\u{200B}y\u{FEFF}z\u{E0041}");
        for needle in ["⟦U+202E⟧", "⟦U+2066⟧", "⟦U+200B⟧", "⟦U+FEFF⟧", "⟦U+E0041⟧"] {
            assert!(out.contains(needle), "{needle} missing in {out:?}");
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn emoji_joiners_and_ordinary_text_are_left_alone() {
        let family = "👨\u{200D}👩\u{200D}👧";
        assert_eq!(clean(family), family);
        assert_eq!(clean("日本語 עברית e\u{301}"), "日本語 עברית e\u{301}");
    }

    #[test]
    fn tabs_returns_and_newlines() {
        assert_eq!(clean("a\tb"), "a    b");
        assert_eq!(clean("a\r\nb"), "a␍\nb");
        assert_eq!(one_line("a\nb"), "a⏎b");
    }

    #[test]
    fn truncate_respects_display_width_and_never_splits_a_cluster() {
        assert_eq!(truncate("hello", 10), "hello");
        assert_eq!(truncate("hello world", 6), "hello…");
        let t = truncate("日本語テキスト", 7);
        assert!(width(&t) <= 7 && t.ends_with('…'), "{t}");
        let t = truncate("e\u{301}e\u{301}e\u{301}", 2);
        assert!(!t.starts_with('\u{301}') && width(&t) <= 2, "{t:?}");
        assert_eq!(truncate("abc", 0), "");
        assert_eq!(truncate("日本", 1), "…");
    }

    #[test]
    fn pad_to_gives_exact_width() {
        assert_eq!(width(&pad_to("日本", 6)), 6);
        assert_eq!(width(&pad_to("abcdefgh", 4)), 4);
    }

    #[test]
    fn random_input_is_always_safe_and_fits() {
        use rand::{rngs::StdRng, Rng, SeedableRng};
        let pool: Vec<char> = "ab 日本\n\t\r\x1b\x07\u{9b}\u{202e}\u{200b}\u{200d}é\u{301}👨".chars().collect();
        let mut rng = StdRng::seed_from_u64(7);
        for _ in 0..5_000 {
            let n = rng.gen_range(0..60);
            let s: String = (0..n).map(|_| pool[rng.gen_range(0..pool.len())]).collect();
            assert!(no_control(&clean(&s)));
            let max = rng.gen_range(0..30);
            assert!(width(&truncate(&one_line(&s), max)) <= max);
        }
    }
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement `sanitize.rs`** (put above the test module)

```rust
//! Text that comes from outside (clips, device names, log lines, file contents) is untrusted: it must
//! never act on the terminal and never reorder or hide what the person reads. Everything shown on
//! screen goes through [`clean`] or [`one_line`] first.
use unicode_segmentation::UnicodeSegmentation;
use unicode_width::UnicodeWidthStr;

fn marker(c: char) -> String {
    format!("⟦U+{:04X}⟧", c as u32)
}

/// Characters that are shown as a visible marker instead of being passed on.
fn is_marked(c: char) -> bool {
    matches!(c as u32,
        0x0080..=0x009F            // C1 controls: CSI, OSC, ... in their 8-bit form
        | 0x061C | 0x200B | 0x200E | 0x200F | 0x2060 | 0xFEFF
        | 0x202A..=0x202E          // bidirectional embeddings and overrides
        | 0x2066..=0x2069          // bidirectional isolates
        | 0xE0000..=0xE007F        // tag characters (invisible "hidden text")
    )
}

/// Make `input` safe to draw. Newlines are kept; tabs become four spaces; carriage returns, C0 controls
/// and DEL become their visible "control picture" (`␛`, `␇`, `␡`, ...); C1 controls, bidi controls,
/// zero-width spaces, BOM and tag characters become `⟦U+XXXX⟧`. Zero-width joiners stay (emoji need them).
pub fn clean(input: &str) -> String {
    let mut out = String::with_capacity(input.len());
    for c in input.chars() {
        match c {
            '\n' => out.push('\n'),
            '\t' => out.push_str("    "),
            '\r' => out.push('␍'),
            '\u{7F}' => out.push('␡'),
            c if (c as u32) < 0x20 => out.push(char::from_u32(0x2400 + c as u32).unwrap_or('�')),
            c if is_marked(c) => out.push_str(&marker(c)),
            c => out.push(c),
        }
    }
    out
}

/// [`clean`], then every newline shown as `⏎` so the result is a single line.
pub fn one_line(input: &str) -> String {
    clean(input).replace('\n', "⏎")
}

/// Display width in terminal columns.
pub fn width(s: &str) -> usize {
    UnicodeWidthStr::width(s)
}

/// Cut `s` to at most `max_cols` columns, ending with `…` when something was dropped. Works on
/// grapheme clusters, so a letter and its combining accent, or an emoji sequence, are never split.
pub fn truncate(s: &str, max_cols: usize) -> String {
    if max_cols == 0 {
        return String::new();
    }
    if width(s) <= max_cols {
        return s.to_string();
    }
    let mut out = String::new();
    let mut used = 0;
    for g in s.graphemes(true) {
        let w = width(g);
        if used + w + 1 > max_cols {
            break;
        }
        out.push_str(g);
        used += w;
    }
    out.push('…');
    out
}

/// Exactly `cols` columns: truncated or padded with spaces.
pub fn pad_to(s: &str, cols: usize) -> String {
    let t = truncate(s, cols);
    let pad = cols.saturating_sub(width(&t));
    format!("{t}{}", " ".repeat(pad))
}
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Implement `fmt.rs`** (above its tests)

```rust
//! Small formatting helpers shared by the screens.

fn group(n: usize) -> String {
    let s = n.to_string();
    let mut out = String::new();
    for (i, ch) in s.chars().enumerate() {
        if i > 0 && (s.len() - i) % 3 == 0 {
            out.push(',');
        }
        out.push(ch);
    }
    out
}

/// Exact below 10 KiB (`1,234 B`), then one decimal in KiB/MiB/GiB.
pub fn bytes(n: usize) -> String {
    const K: f64 = 1024.0;
    if n < 10 * 1024 {
        return format!("{} B", group(n));
    }
    let f = n as f64;
    if f < K * K {
        format!("{:.1} KiB", f / K)
    } else if f < K * K * K {
        format!("{:.1} MiB", f / (K * K))
    } else {
        format!("{:.1} GiB", f / (K * K * K))
    }
}

pub fn ago(secs: u64) -> String {
    match secs {
        0..=1 => "just now".to_string(),
        2..=59 => format!("{secs}s ago"),
        60..=3599 => format!("{} min ago", secs / 60),
        3600..=86_399 => format!("{} h ago", secs / 3600),
        _ => format!("{} d ago", secs / 86_400),
    }
}

pub fn ago_ms(now_ms: u64, then_ms: u64) -> String {
    ago(now_ms.saturating_sub(then_ms) / 1000)
}

pub fn mmss(secs: u64) -> String {
    format!("{}:{:02}", secs / 60, secs % 60)
}

/// A countdown: `1:30`, `1 h 05 min`, or `until you resume` for the "no end" pause.
pub fn remaining(secs: u64) -> String {
    if secs > 365 * 24 * 3600 {
        "until you resume".to_string()
    } else if secs >= 3600 {
        format!("{} h {:02} min", secs / 3600, (secs % 3600) / 60)
    } else {
        mmss(secs)
    }
}
```

```rust
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use rand::{rngs::StdRng, Rng, SeedableRng};

    fn typed(s: &str) -> TextBuf {
        let mut b = TextBuf::new(1 << 20);
        b.insert_str(s);
        b

    #[test]

        let mut b = TextBuf::new(100);
        b.insert_str("ab");
        b.newline();
        b.insert_str("cd");
        assert_eq!(b.text(), "ab\ncd");
        assert_eq!(b.cursor(), (1, 2));

    }

    #[test]

    }

    #[test]
    fn backspace_removes_a_whole_grapheme_and_joins_lines() {
        let mut b = typed("ae\u{301}");
        b.backspace();
        assert_eq!(b.text(), "a");
        let mut b = typed("a\nb");
        b.backspace();
        b.backspace();
        assert_eq!(b.text(), "a");
        assert_eq!(b.cursor(), (0, 1));
        let mut b = typed("👨\u{200D}👩\u{200D}👧x");
        b.backspace();
        b.backspace();
        assert_eq!(b.text(), "");
    }

        b.up();
        b.end();
        b.delete();

    #[test]

        b.up();
        b.end();
        b.down();
        assert_eq!(b.cursor(), (1, 1));
        b.left();
        b.left();

    #[test]
    fn capacity_is_respected_and_never_splits_a_character() {
        let mut b = TextBuf::new(4);
        assert_eq!(b.insert_str("日本"), 3);
        assert_eq!(b.text(), "日");
        assert_eq!(b.insert_str("ab"), 1);

    #[test]

        assert_eq!(b.text(), "one ");

        b.left();
        b.insert_str("b\nc");
        assert_eq!(b.text(), "ab\ncd");
        assert_eq!(b.cursor(), (1, 1));
    }

    #[test]

        let mut b = typed("a\nb");
        b.clear();
        assert!(b.is_empty());
        assert_eq!(b.cursor(), (0, 0));
    }

    #[test]
    fn random_operations_keep_every_invariant() {
        let pool = [
            "a", "é", "e\u{301}", "日", "👨\u{200D}👩\u{200D}👧", "\n", "\r\n", " ", "\t", "xyz", "\u{200D}",
        ];
        let mut rng = StdRng::seed_from_u64(42);
        for _ in 0..200 {
            let cap = rng.gen_range(0..200);
            let mut b = TextBuf::new(cap);
            for _ in 0..200 {
                match rng.gen_range(0..12) {
                    0..=2 => {
                        b.insert_str(pool[rng.gen_range(0..pool.len())]);
                    }
                    3 => b.newline(),
                    4 => b.backspace(),
                    5 => b.delete(),
                    6 => b.left(),
                    7 => b.right(),
                    8 => b.up(),
                    9 => b.down(),
                    10 => b.home(),
                    _ => {

                            b.end()
                        } else {
                            b.delete_word_back()
                        }
                    }
                }
                assert!(b.byte_len() <= cap, "{} > {cap}", b.byte_len());
                assert_eq!(b.text().len(), b.byte_len());
                let (r, c) = b.cursor();
                assert!(r < b.lines().len());
                assert!(c <= gcount(&b.lines()[r]));
            }
        }
    }
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement** (above the test module)

```rust
//! A small multi-line text buffer for the editors (Send, ticket and filter fields). Lines are
//! `String`s; the cursor counts grapheme clusters, so an accented letter or an emoji sequence is one
//! step. The buffer never exceeds `max_bytes` (counting the newlines) and never splits a character.
use unicode_segmentation::UnicodeSegmentation;

#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct TextBuf {
    lines: Vec<String>,
    row: usize,
    col: usize,
    max_bytes: usize,
}

fn gcount(s: &str) -> usize {
    s.graphemes(true).count()
}

/// Byte offset of grapheme `g` (the end of the string when `g` is past the last one).
fn boff(s: &str, g: usize) -> usize {
    s.grapheme_indices(true).nth(g).map(|(i, _)| i).unwrap_or(s.len())
}

impl TextBuf {
    pub fn new(max_bytes: usize) -> Self {
        Self { lines: vec![String::new()], row: 0, col: 0, max_bytes }
    }

    /// A buffer holding `text` (cut at capacity) with the cursor at the start.
    pub fn from_text(text: &str, max_bytes: usize) -> Self {
        let mut b = Self::new(max_bytes);
        b.insert_str(text);
        b.row = 0;
        b.col = 0;
        b
    }

    pub fn text(&self) -> String {
        self.lines.join("\n")
    }

    pub fn byte_len(&self) -> usize {
        self.lines.iter().map(|l| l.len()).sum::<usize>() + self.lines.len().saturating_sub(1)
    }

    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
        self.lines.len() == 1 && self.lines[0].is_empty()
    }

    pub fn lines(&self) -> &[String] {
        &self.lines
    }

    /// `(row, column)`, the column counted in grapheme clusters.
    pub fn cursor(&self) -> (usize, usize) {
        (self.row, self.col)
    }

    pub fn max_bytes(&self) -> usize {
        self.max_bytes
    }

    fn clamp(&mut self) {
        self.row = self.row.min(self.lines.len() - 1);
        self.col = self.col.min(gcount(&self.lines[self.row]));
    }

    /// Insert at the cursor. `\r\n` and `\r` become `\n`. Returns the number of bytes inserted
    /// (less than `s.len()` when the capacity ran out).
    pub fn insert_str(&mut self, s: &str) -> usize {
        let normalized = s.replace("\r\n", "\n").replace('\r', "\n");
        let mut room = self.max_bytes.saturating_sub(self.byte_len());
        let mut take = String::new();
        let mut inserted = 0;
        for ch in normalized.chars() {
            let n = ch.len_utf8();
            if n > room {
                break;
            }
            room -= n;
            take.push(ch);
            inserted += n;
        }
        let at = boff(&self.lines[self.row], self.col);
        let tail = self.lines[self.row].split_off(at);
        let mut pieces = take.split('\n');
        self.lines[self.row].push_str(pieces.next().unwrap_or(""));
        let mut cur = self.row;
        for p in pieces {
            cur += 1;
            self.lines.insert(cur, p.to_string());
        }
        let end_byte = self.lines[cur].len();
        self.lines[cur].push_str(&tail);
        self.row = cur;
        self.col = gcount(&self.lines[cur][..end_byte]);
        inserted
    }

    pub fn insert_char(&mut self, c: char) {
        let mut tmp = [0u8; 4];
        self.insert_str(c.encode_utf8(&mut tmp));
    }

    pub fn newline(&mut self) {
        self.insert_str("\n");
    }

    pub fn backspace(&mut self) {
        if self.col > 0 {
            let line = &mut self.lines[self.row];
            let start = boff(line, self.col - 1);
            let end = boff(line, self.col);
            line.replace_range(start..end, "");
            self.col -= 1;
        } else if self.row > 0 {
            let current = self.lines.remove(self.row);
            self.row -= 1;
            self.col = gcount(&self.lines[self.row]);
            self.lines[self.row].push_str(&current);
        }
        self.clamp();
    }

    pub fn delete(&mut self) {
        let n = gcount(&self.lines[self.row]);
        if self.col < n {
            let line = &mut self.lines[self.row];
            let start = boff(line, self.col);
            let end = boff(line, self.col + 1);
            line.replace_range(start..end, "");
        } else if self.row + 1 < self.lines.len() {
            let next = self.lines.remove(self.row + 1);
            self.lines[self.row].push_str(&next);
        }
        self.clamp();
    }

    pub fn left(&mut self) {
        if self.col > 0 {
            self.col -= 1;
        } else if self.row > 0 {
            self.row -= 1;
            self.col = gcount(&self.lines[self.row]);
        }
    }

    pub fn right(&mut self) {
        if self.col < gcount(&self.lines[self.row]) {
            self.col += 1;
        } else if self.row + 1 < self.lines.len() {
            self.row += 1;
            self.col = 0;
        }
    }

    pub fn up(&mut self) {
        if self.row > 0 {
            self.row -= 1;
            self.clamp();
        }
    }

    pub fn down(&mut self) {
        if self.row + 1 < self.lines.len() {
            self.row += 1;
            self.clamp();
        }
    }

    pub fn home(&mut self) {
        self.col = 0;
    }

    pub fn end(&mut self) {
        self.col = gcount(&self.lines[self.row]);
    }

    /// Delete the whitespace before the cursor and then the word before that; at the start of a
    /// line it joins with the previous line like Backspace.
    pub fn delete_word_back(&mut self) {
        if self.col == 0 {
            self.backspace();
            return;
        }
        let gs: Vec<&str> = self.lines[self.row].graphemes(true).collect();
        let mut i = self.col.min(gs.len());
        while i > 0 && gs[i - 1].trim().is_empty() {
            i -= 1;
        }
        while i > 0 && !gs[i - 1].trim().is_empty() {
            i -= 1;
        }
        let start = boff(&self.lines[self.row], i);
        let end = boff(&self.lines[self.row], self.col);
        self.lines[self.row].replace_range(start..end, "");
        self.col = i;
        self.clamp();
    }

    pub fn clear(&mut self) {
        self.lines = vec![String::new()];
        self.row = 0;
        self.col = 0;
    }
}
```

```rust
#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use klip_core::KlipPaths;
    use ratatui::style::Color;

    fn env(vars: &[(&str, &str)]) -> TuiEnv {
        let dir = std::env::temp_dir().join("klip-tui-theme-test");
        TuiEnv::from_vars(KlipPaths::from_dir(dir), |k| {
            vars.iter().find(|(n, _)| *n == k).map(|(_, v)| v.to_string())
        })
    }

    #[test]
    fn truecolor_uses_the_brand_colours() {
        let t = Theme::from_env(&env(&[("COLORTERM", "truecolor"), ("TERM", "xterm-256color")]));
        assert_eq!(t.signal, Color::Rgb(200, 242, 90));
        assert!(!t.plain);
    }

    #[test]
    fn light_theme_uses_the_dark_green_signal() {
        let t = Theme::from_env(&env(&[("COLORTERM", "24bit"), ("KLIP_THEME", "light")]));
        assert_eq!(t.signal, Color::Rgb(77, 122, 0));
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_256_colour_terminal_gets_indexed_colours() {
        let t = Theme::from_env(&env(&[("TERM", "xterm-256color")]));
        assert_eq!(t.signal, Color::Indexed(192));
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_basic_terminal_gets_the_16_colours() {
        let t = Theme::from_env(&env(&[("TERM", "xterm")]));
        assert_eq!(t.signal, Color::Green);
    }

    #[test]
    fn no_color_means_plain_and_wins_over_everything() {
        let t = Theme::from_env(&env(&[("NO_COLOR", "1"), ("COLORTERM", "truecolor")]));
        assert!(t.plain);
        assert_eq!(t.signal, Color::Reset);
        let t = Theme::from_env(&env(&[("NO_COLOR", ""), ("COLORTERM", "truecolor")]));
        assert!(!t.plain, "an empty NO_COLOR does not count");
    }
}
```

```rust
#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use klip_core::{DaemonConfig, KlipPaths, RelayMode};
    use std::io::Write;

    fn tmp(tag: &str) -> PathBuf {
        let d = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("klip-tui-files-{}-{tag}", std::process::id()));
        let _ = fs::remove_dir_all(&d);
        fs::create_dir_all(&d).unwrap();
        d
    }

    #[test]
    fn config_is_written_atomically_with_mode_0600_and_reads_back() {
        let paths = KlipPaths::from_dir(tmp("cfg"));
        let cfg = DaemonConfig {
            device_name: "Laptop".into(),
            relay: RelayMode::Lan,
            max_history_items: 20,
            ..DaemonConfig::default()
        };
        write_config_atomic(&paths, &cfg).unwrap();
        let back = DaemonConfig::load(&paths).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(back.device_name, "Laptop");
        assert_eq!(back.relay, RelayMode::Lan);
        assert_eq!(back.max_history_items, 20);
        use std::os::unix::fs::PermissionsExt;
        let mode = fs::metadata(paths.config_file()).unwrap().permissions().mode() & 0o777;
        assert_eq!(mode, 0o600);
        assert!(!paths.dir().join(".config.json.tmp").exists(), "temp file must be renamed away");
    }

    #[test]
    fn an_invalid_config_is_refused_and_the_old_file_is_untouched() {
        let paths = KlipPaths::from_dir(tmp("badcfg"));
        write_config_atomic(&paths, &DaemonConfig::default()).unwrap();
        let before = fs::read_to_string(paths.config_file()).unwrap();
        let bad = DaemonConfig { max_clip_bytes: 0, ..DaemonConfig::default() };
        assert!(write_config_atomic(&paths, &bad).is_err());
        assert_eq!(fs::read_to_string(paths.config_file()).unwrap(), before);
    }

    #[test]
    fn log_first_read_returns_the_tail_from_a_line_boundary() {
        let dir = tmp("log1");
        let path = dir.join("daemon.log");
        let mut f = fs::File::create(&path).unwrap();
        for i in 0..20_000 {
            writeln!(f, "line number {i:06} with some padding to make it longer").unwrap();
        }
        let chunk = read_log_chunk(&path, None).unwrap();
        assert!(!chunk.missing && !chunk.reset);
        assert!(chunk.text.len() as u64 <= LOG_INITIAL_BYTES);
        assert!(chunk.text.starts_with("line number "), "starts mid-line: {:?}", &chunk.text[..30]);
        assert!(chunk.text.ends_with('\n'));
        assert_eq!(chunk.new_offset, fs::metadata(&path).unwrap().len());
    }

    #[test]
    fn log_follow_returns_only_new_complete_lines() {
        let dir = tmp("log2");
        let path = dir.join("daemon.log");
        fs::write(&path, "one\ntwo\n").unwrap();
        let first = read_log_chunk(&path, None).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(first.text, "one\ntwo\n");
        let mut f = fs::OpenOptions::new().append(true).open(&path).unwrap();
        f.write_all(b"three\npart").unwrap();
        let second = read_log_chunk(&path, Some(first.new_offset)).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(second.text, "three\n", "an unfinished line waits for its newline");
        f.write_all(b"ial\n").unwrap();
        let third = read_log_chunk(&path, Some(second.new_offset)).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(third.text, "partial\n");
    }

    #[test]
    fn log_truncation_and_missing_file_are_reported_not_fatal() {
        let dir = tmp("log3");
        let path = dir.join("daemon.log");
        let missing = read_log_chunk(&path, None).unwrap();
        assert!(missing.missing && missing.text.is_empty());
        fs::write(&path, "a long first line that will be cut\n").unwrap();
        let first = read_log_chunk(&path, None).unwrap();
        fs::write(&path, "new\n").unwrap();
        let again = read_log_chunk(&path, Some(first.new_offset)).unwrap();
        assert!(again.reset);
        assert_eq!(again.text, "new\n");
    }

    #[test]
    fn log_with_binary_garbage_is_converted_lossily() {
        let dir = tmp("log4");
        let path = dir.join("daemon.log");
        fs::write(&path, b"ok\n\xff\xfe\x00bad\n").unwrap();
        let chunk = read_log_chunk(&path, None).unwrap();
        assert!(chunk.text.contains("ok\n") && chunk.text.contains("bad"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn service_state_follows_the_unit_file() {
        let home = tmp("svc");
        assert_eq!(service_state(Some(&home)), ServiceState::NotInstalled);
        let unit = service_unit_path(&home).expect("linux/macOS have a unit path");
        fs::create_dir_all(unit.parent().unwrap()).unwrap();
        fs::write(&unit, "[Unit]\n").unwrap();
        assert_eq!(service_state(Some(&home)), ServiceState::Installed(unit));
        assert_eq!(service_state(None), ServiceState::Unsupported);
    }

    #[test]
    fn text_files_load_with_size_and_encoding_checks() {
        let dir = tmp("load");
        let ok = dir.join("a.txt");
        fs::write(&ok, "héllo\n").unwrap();
        assert_eq!(load_text_file(ok.to_str().unwrap(), 1000).unwrap(), "héllo\n");
        let big = dir.join("big.txt");
        fs::write(&big, vec![b'x'; 2000]).unwrap();
        assert!(load_text_file(big.to_str().unwrap(), 1000).unwrap_err().contains("too large"));
        let bin = dir.join("bin");
        fs::write(&bin, [0xffu8, 0xfe, 0x00]).unwrap();
        assert!(load_text_file(bin.to_str().unwrap(), 1000).unwrap_err().contains("UTF-8"));
        assert!(load_text_file(dir.join("nope").to_str().unwrap(), 1000).is_err());
        assert!(load_text_file(dir.to_str().unwrap(), 1000).is_err(), "a directory is not a file");
    }
}
```
